        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Wan2GP Fork Migration — Sprint 4 (Patch Consolidation, Upstream PRs, Sustainability)

## Overview
Sprint 4 closes out the migration by (a) consolidating runtime fork behavior into a single `wgp_patches.py` registry so `get_wgp_patch_state()` is the one place to see what the fork mutates, (b) drafting upstream PR bodies for the 118 bucket-(b) rows in `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` grouped by theme (~13 PRs), (c) landing a weekly drift-detection workflow per SD-008, and (d) publishing the rebase runbook per SD-007.

Key repo shape:
- `source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py` already has `_register_patch_application`, `get_wgp_patch_state`, `clear_wgp_patch_context`, `rollback_wgp_patches`, and a context-scoped registry (`_PATCH_CONTEXT_STATE`, `_PATCH_CONTEXT_ROLLBACKS`). Today it wraps seven module-level patches.
- `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py:201-222` defines `begin_runtime_model_patch` / `apply_runtime_model_patch` / `rollback_runtime_model_patch`. They mutate `runtime.models_def[name]` + `wan_model.model_def` via a `_RuntimePatchTransaction` snapshot, but no production code calls them — they are the "split runtime patch state" Sprint 4 must fold in.
- Patch application seam: `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:265-266` (`apply_all_wgp_patches(wgp, self.wan_root)`).
- Baseline tag `wan2gp-mig-sprint-4-baseline` already exists.

Executor constraints (learned from Sprints 1-3):
- Cannot write `.git/` or push remotes — orchestrator opens PRs and manages baseline tags.
- `uv sync` fails on darwin-arm64 (decord wheel gap). `source.media.structure.{loading,frame_ops,download}` import decord lazily, so the 8-file regression suite runs on a minimal smoke venv as long as no test exercises those lazy paths.
- Real `WanOrchestrator` bootstrap imports `Wan2GP/wgp.py` which eagerly imports decord, so the end-to-end patch-lifecycle smoke is orchestrator-side only; executor ships a stub-form pytest proof.
- Do not commit. Stage everything for user review.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the existing patch registry and runtime primitives (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`, `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `wgp_patches.py:27-114` (registry + rollback primitives) and `wgp_patches.py:536-580` (`apply_all_wgp_patches`) to confirm the registration shape and context-id contract.
2. **Read** `runtime_registry.py:184-222` (`_RuntimePatchTransaction`, `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch`) and `wgp_bridge.py:19-21,94-126` to confirm the primitives are already re-exported via the bridge.
3. **Confirm** no production caller exists today (only `tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py`) — this is the split registry Sprint 4 folds in.

### Step 2: Extend `wgp_patches.py` with a runtime-model-patch wrapper (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** imports for `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch` from `source.runtime.wgp_bridge`.
2. **Implement** `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch(model_name, patch_values, *, patch_name, context_id=None)` next to `_register_patch_application`:
   - Call `begin_runtime_model_patch(model_name, keys=tuple(patch_values.keys()))`.
   - Call `apply_runtime_model_patch(transaction, patch_values)`.
   - Register in the single registry via `_register_patch_application(patch_name, target_key=f"model_def:{model_name}", applied=True, context_id=context_id, rollback=lambda: _rollback_runtime_model_definition(transaction, len(patch_values)))` where the inner function wraps `rollback_runtime_model_patch(transaction)` and returns an int counter consistent with existing rollbacks.
   - Return `True`.
3. **Do not** wire a concrete per-model override (e.g., `svi2pro`, `res_2s` flag) into `apply_all_wgp_patches` this sprint — scope is to expose the primitive in the single registry. Q1 covers whether a concrete consumer should be added.
4. **Preserve** the existing `apply_all_wgp_patches` return-shape and summary logging; no new entry appended.

### Step 3: Prove the single-registry invariant (`tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_registers_in_single_registry`:
   - Build a `_FakeRuntime`-style stub matching `tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py` and monkeypatch `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module` to return it.
   - Call the new wrapper with `patch_name="ltx2_runtime_override_smoke"`, `model_name="ltx2_22B"`, `patch_values={"svi2pro": True}`.
   - Assert `runtime.models_def["ltx2_22B"]["svi2pro"] is True`.
   - Assert `wgp_patches.get_wgp_patch_state()["ltx2_runtime_override_smoke"]["model_def:ltx2_22B"]["applied"] is True`.
   - Call `rollback_wgp_patches(patch_name="ltx2_runtime_override_smoke")` and assert the key is gone from both `models_def` and the registry.
2. **Do not** introduce a parallel test file — keep all patch-state contracts co-located.

### Step 4: Draft bucket-(b) upstream PR bodies (`scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/` with a `README.md` stating drafts only, listing theme → row mapping, and noting that the orchestrator actually opens PRs.
2. **Write** one markdown body per theme (≈13 files), named `NN-<theme-slug>.md`, sections: Title, Summary, Affected files (grep-derived), Manual diff review pointer to the CSV's `manual diff review before resync` entry, Test plan ("`uv run --python 3.10 pytest` + upstream smoke; maintainer-directed").
3. **Themes** (from `cut -d, -f2 docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | sort | uniq -c`): `ltx2_hotzone_drift` (20), `preset_config_drift` (15), `wan_runtime_drift` (13), `tts_implementation_drift` (11), `shared_runtime_drift` (9), `model_implementation_drift` (9), `shared_utility_drift` (8), `llm_engine_drift` (8), `project_metadata_drift` (6), `plugin_integration_drift` (6), `documentation_drift` (5), `installer_script_drift` (3), `gradio_ui_drift` (3), `content_drift` (2). Q3 covers finer splits.
4. **Do not** run `gh pr create` or any remote command.

### Step 5: Land drift CI per SD-008 (`.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml`:
   - `on.schedule` weekly (e.g., `cron: '0 12 * * 1'`) plus `on.workflow_dispatch`.
   - `jobs.drift-check` on `ubuntu-latest`:
     - `actions/checkout@v4` with `submodules: true`.
     - Fetch upstream `deepbeepmeep/Wan2GP` default-branch SHA via `gh api` or `git ls-remote`.
     - Compare against `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD`.
     - Compute commits-behind via `git -C Wan2GP rev-list --count $PINNED..$UPSTREAM` after adding upstream remote.
     - Report via `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`; per SD-008 "reports drift for review", do not fail the job on drift (only on command failure). Q2 covers fail-vs-report.
   - `permissions: { contents: read, pull-requests: read }`.
2. **Dry-run** the diff snippet locally against the current submodule pointer; verify pointer matches `LOCAL_SHA=181bb71a21008032e4771e11663f33e4489c4512` from `scripts/sprint3/_handoff/orchestrator_handoff_note_2.txt`.
3. **Validate** yaml syntax with a lightweight `yaml.safe_load` check.

### Step 6: Publish the rebase runbook per SD-007 (`docs/wan2gp-rebase-runbook.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/wan2gp-rebase-runbook.md` with: Cadence (monthly + event triggers), Pre-flight (baseline tag, green suite), Fork rebase procedure in `banodoco/Wan2GP`, Submodule bump in `reigh-worker`, Post-bump verification (8-file suite + strict path smoke + patch-lifecycle smoke), Rollback (orchestrator-only, with executor limitation called out), Drift CI interpretation.
2. **Cross-link** to `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` and `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`.

### Step 7: Verification gates
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Cheap focused tests** first:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py`
2. **Full 8-file regression suite**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py tests/test_travel_guidance_ic_lora_override.py tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py`
   - If `uv run` fails on darwin-arm64, fall back to `.venv/bin/python -m pytest …`; the 8 tests do not exercise the lazy decord imports.
3. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (executor, stub form)**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py::test_runtime_model_definition_patch_registers_in_single_registry`
4. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (orchestrator, full bootstrap form)**: ship `scripts/sprint4/patch_lifecycle_smoke.py` that drives `WanOrchestrator` to the `apply_all_wgp_patches` line and prints `get_wgp_patch_state()` as JSON. Document as linux-only in the runbook.
5. **Drift CI once-through**: orchestrator triggers via `workflow_dispatch` after merge; executor cannot.
6. **Strict path-contract smoke**: re-run the Sprint 2 one-liner from Section 4 of the migration plan.
7. **Doc cross-reference**: verify runbook references resolve to existing files.
8. **Leave staged**: `git add` the new/changed files; do NOT commit. Hand a staged-file list to the user.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (audit) → Step 2 (wrapper) → Step 3 (lifecycle test).
2. Steps 4 (PR drafts), 5 (drift CI), 6 (runbook) in parallel after Step 3.
3. Step 7 verification last.

## Validation Order
1. Focused: `test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py` + `test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py` + `test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py`.
2. Broad: 8-file regression suite.
3. Strict path-contract smoke.
4. Yaml-load check for the drift workflow; dry-run the drift diff logic locally.
5. Visual review of the four drafted deliverables + staged diff hand-off.

        Robustness level:

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Wan2GP Fork Migration — Sprint 4 (Patch Consolidation, Upstream PRs, Sustainability)

## Overview
Sprint 4 closes out the migration by (a) consolidating runtime fork behavior into a single `wgp_patches.py` registry AND wiring a concrete runtime-model-definition patch into the bootstrap so `get_wgp_patch_state()` after `apply_all_wgp_patches()` proves the single-registry invariant in the real production path, (b) drafting upstream PR bodies for the 118 bucket-(b) rows in `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` grouped into the 14 distinct hunk themes already present in the CSV, (c) landing a weekly drift-detection workflow per SD-008, and (d) publishing the rebase runbook per SD-007. Orchestrator-owned handoff is explicitly documented for the gates the executor cannot run (PR open, drift-CI once-through, baseline tag/commit).

Key repo shape:
- `source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py` already has `_register_patch_application`, `get_wgp_patch_state`, `clear_wgp_patch_context`, `rollback_wgp_patches`, `_PATCH_CONTEXT_STATE`, `_PATCH_CONTEXT_ROLLBACKS`. Today `apply_all_wgp_patches` (`wgp_patches.py:536-580`) records seven module-level patches.
- `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py:184-222` defines `_RuntimePatchTransaction`, `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch`. Today no production caller touches them.
- Patch application seam: `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:265-266` (`apply_all_wgp_patches(wgp, self.wan_root)`). The authoritative Sprint 4 gate drives bootstrap to this line and then inspects `get_wgp_patch_state()`.
- Baseline tag `wan2gp-mig-sprint-4-baseline` already exists.
- `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` has 118 upstreamable rows splitting into **14 distinct hunk themes** (`grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | cut -d, -f2 | sort -u | wc -l` = 14).

Executor constraints (Sprints 1-3):
- Cannot write `.git/` or push remotes — orchestrator opens PRs, tags baselines, triggers `workflow_dispatch`.
- `uv sync` fails on darwin-arm64 (decord==0.6.0 wheel gap). `source.media.structure.*` imports decord lazily, so the 8-file regression suite runs on the existing `.venv` smoke venv.
- Real `WanOrchestrator` bootstrap imports `Wan2GP/wgp.py` which eagerly imports decord — full bootstrap smoke is orchestrator/linux-only.
- Do not commit. Stage everything for user review.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the existing patch registry, runtime primitives, and bootstrap seam (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`, `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py`, `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `wgp_patches.py:27-114` (registry + rollback primitives), `wgp_patches.py:183-229` (`apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` — authoritative idempotency-guard pattern), `wgp_patches.py:536-580` (`apply_all_wgp_patches`).
2. **Read** `runtime_registry.py:184-222` + `wgp_bridge.py:19-21,94-126` to confirm the three primitives are re-exported.
3. **Read** `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:245-267` to confirm `apply_all_wgp_patches(wgp, self.wan_root)` is the single bootstrap seam; note that `wgp.models_def` is populated before this line, so the extension patch can safely iterate over it.

### Step 2: Extend `wgp_patches.py` with an idempotent runtime-model-def wrapper and a concrete bootstrap-wired fork-marker patch (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Import** `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch` from `source.runtime.wgp_bridge`.
2. **Implement** `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch(model_name, patch_values, *, patch_name, context_id=None)` with an explicit idempotency guard that mirrors `apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` (`wgp_patches.py:197-204`):
   - Compute `target_key = f"model_def:{model_name}"`.
   - **Guard:** `existing = get_wgp_patch_state(context_id=context_id).get(patch_name, {})`; if `target_key in existing and existing[target_key].get("applied")` → return `True` immediately (do NOT open a second transaction; the first rollback closure is preserved). This fixes FLAG-002 / correctness / callers-2.
   - Otherwise call `transaction = begin_runtime_model_patch(model_name, keys=tuple(patch_values.keys()))`, then `apply_runtime_model_patch(transaction, patch_values)`.
   - Register via `_register_patch_application(patch_name, target_key, applied=True, context_id=context_id, rollback=_make_runtime_model_def_rollback(transaction, len(patch_values)))` where the helper returns a closure that calls `rollback_runtime_model_patch(transaction)` and returns the int count (match existing rollback-return contract).
   - Return `True`.
3. **Implement** a concrete bootstrap-wired patch `apply_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers_patch(wgp_module, *, context_id=None)` that:
   - Iterates `wgp_module.models_def.keys()` filtering to `k for k in keys if isinstance(k, str) and k.startswith("ltx2")`.
   - For each matching key, calls `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch(k, {"_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch": True}, patch_name="ltx2_runtime_fork_markers", context_id=context_id)`.
   - Returns `True` if at least one entry was touched OR if no ltx2 entries existed (treat empty-models_def as soft-OK for smoke mode); returns `False` only on exception.
   - Rationale: `_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch` is a private marker key with leading underscore → zero collision risk with upstream model-def fields and no observable generation-path effect. It is the minimum viable concrete patch that satisfies the authoritative gate "bootstrap smoke inspects `get_wgp_patch_state()` and sees a runtime-model entry"; SD-005 concrete defaults (`res_2s`, `self_refiner`) can replace it in a later sprint without widening Sprint 4 scope into product behavior.
4. **Wire** the new patch into `apply_all_wgp_patches` (`wgp_patches.py:552-560`):
   ```python
   results["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"] = apply_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers_patch(wgp_module, context_id=context_id)
   ```
   After this, the final `_register_patch_application` loop over `results` at `wgp_patches.py:571-578` also registers the aggregate result under the `wgp_module:<id>` target key, and the per-model entries registered by the wrapper live under the same `_PATCH_CONTEXT_STATE[context_id]["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"]` bucket — single registry. This fixes FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1 / scope-1 / scope-2 / callers-1.
5. **Preserve** `apply_all_wgp_patches` return-dict shape and the existing `successful`/`failed` summary logging; the new key just slots into `results`.

### Step 3: Prove the single-registry invariant + idempotency + context_id scoping (`tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_registers_in_single_registry` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`:
   - Build a `_FakeRuntime` stub matching `tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py:10-21` (models_def + loaded wan_model.model_def) and monkeypatch `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module` to return it.
   - Call wrapper with `patch_name="ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"`, `model_name="ltx2_22B"`, `patch_values={"_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch": True}`.
   - Assert `runtime.models_def["ltx2_22B"]["_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch"] is True`.
   - Assert `get_wgp_patch_state()["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"]["model_def:ltx2_22B"]["applied"] is True` (single registry).
   - Call `rollback_wgp_patches(patch_name="ltx2_runtime_fork_markers")` → assert restored ≥ 1, key gone from both `models_def` and registry.
2. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_is_idempotent`:
   - After a first successful apply, mutate `runtime.models_def["ltx2_22B"]["_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch"] = "intruder"` (simulate external change between calls).
   - Call the wrapper a second time with the same `patch_name`/`model_name`/`patch_values`.
   - Assert the guard short-circuited: `runtime.models_def["ltx2_22B"]["_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch"] == "intruder"` (wrapper did NOT open a new transaction or overwrite).
   - Rollback → assert the `"intruder"` value is cleared back to absent (the ORIGINAL first-call transaction snapshot restored pre-patch state, not the intruder state). This pins FLAG-002 fix.
3. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_respects_context_id` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py`:
   - Call the wrapper twice with identical args under two distinct `context_id` values (`"ctx.a"`, `"ctx.b"`).
   - Assert both contexts report the patch via `get_wgp_patch_state(context_id=…)`.
   - Call `clear_wgp_patch_context("ctx.a")` → assert `get_wgp_patch_state(context_id="ctx.a") == {}` and `get_wgp_patch_state(context_id="ctx.b")` still has the entry.
   - Tear down both contexts in a `try/finally`. This pins all_locations-2.
4. **Add** `test_apply_all_wgp_patches_records_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py` (sits next to existing `test_patch_state_is_context_scoped`):
   - Build a `_make_dummy_wgp_module` enriched with `models_def={"ltx2_22B": {}, "ltx2_22B_distilled": {}, "t2v": {}}`.
   - Monkeypatch all module-level patch-apply helpers to return `True` (same pattern as existing test).
   - Monkeypatch `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module` so `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch` sees the same models_def.
   - Call `apply_all_wgp_patches(dummy, ".", context_id="test.bootstrap")`.
   - Assert `get_wgp_patch_state(context_id="test.bootstrap")["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"]` contains BOTH `model_def:ltx2_22B` and `model_def:ltx2_22B_distilled` and NOT `model_def:t2v`. This proves the concrete patch is reachable from the bootstrap path through the same registry as module-level patches.

### Step 4: Draft bucket-(b) upstream PR bodies, one per CSV hunk theme (`scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Derive themes from the CSV itself** (not the plan summary): run `grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | cut -d, -f2 | sort -u` → 14 distinct `hunk` strings. Confirmed counts via `cut -d, -f2 docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | sort | uniq -c` → `ltx2 hot-zone implementation drift` (20), `preset/config drift` (15), `Wan runtime drift` (13), `TTS implementation drift` (11), `shared runtime drift` (9), `model implementation drift` (9), `shared utility drift` (8), `LLM engine drift` (8), `project metadata drift` (6), `plugin integration drift` (6), `documentation drift` (5), `installer/update script drift` (3), `Gradio UI drift` (3), `content drift` (2). This fixes all_locations-1.
2. **Create** `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/` with `README.md` that:
   - States drafts only; orchestrator opens PRs.
   - Maps `<NN>-<slug>.md` → exact `hunk` string from the CSV (no invented grouping).
   - Cites the CSV as the source of truth.
3. **Write 14 markdown files** (`01-ltx2-hotzone-drift.md` … `14-content-drift.md`). Each file:
   - **Title** — e.g., `[Upstreamable] ltx2 hot-zone implementation drift vs upstream (20 files)`.
   - **Summary** — 1-paragraph framing, links to triage row set.
   - **Affected files** — literal list from `grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | grep "<hunk exact>" | cut -d, -f1`.
   - **Verification** — `uv run --python 3.10 pytest` on the Sprint 4 regression suite + maintainer-directed upstream smoke.
   - **Review pointer** — the CSV's `manual diff review before resync` verification entry.
4. **Do not** run `gh pr create` or any remote command.

### Step 5: Land drift CI per SD-008 (`.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml`:
   - `on.schedule: cron: '0 12 * * 1'` (weekly Monday 12:00 UTC) + `on.workflow_dispatch`.
   - `jobs.drift-check` on `ubuntu-latest`:
     - `actions/checkout@v4` with `submodules: true`.
     - Step: `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD > $GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` (pinned SHA).
     - Step: fetch upstream HEAD via `git ls-remote https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/Wan2GP.git HEAD | awk '{print $1}'`.
     - Step: add upstream remote inside submodule, fetch, compute `git -C Wan2GP rev-list --count $PINNED..$UPSTREAM`.
     - Step: append drift summary to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY` (pinned, upstream, commits-behind).
     - **Report-only** per SD-008: do NOT fail on non-zero drift; fail only on git command error.
   - `permissions: { contents: read, pull-requests: read }`.
2. **Dry-run** the diff snippet locally against the current submodule pointer; verify pointer matches `LOCAL_SHA=181bb71a21008032e4771e11663f33e4489c4512` from `scripts/sprint3/_handoff/orchestrator_handoff_note_2.txt`.
3. **Validate** yaml syntax: `.venv/bin/python -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml'))"` (fall back to `actionlint`-in-docker if pyyaml isn't in the smoke venv).
4. **Record** explicitly in the Sprint 4 handoff note (Step 7 sub-step 7) that the "Drift CI runs once successfully" authoritative gate is orchestrator-owned (requires `workflow_dispatch` push — executor cannot). This closes the docs loop on issue_hints-2 while keeping the SD-exec-no-git decision intact.

### Step 6: Publish the rebase runbook per SD-007 (`docs/wan2gp-rebase-runbook.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/wan2gp-rebase-runbook.md` sections: Cadence (monthly + event-triggered), Pre-flight (tag `wan2gp-mig-rebase-YYYY-MM-baseline`, green 8-file suite), Fork rebase procedure in `banodoco/Wan2GP`, Submodule bump in `reigh-worker`, Post-bump verification (8-file suite + strict path smoke + patch-lifecycle smoke both forms), Rollback (orchestrator-only — flagged with explicit SD-exec-no-git reference), Drift CI interpretation (including that `workflow_dispatch` once-through is orchestrator-owned).
2. **Cross-link** to `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` and `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`.

### Step 7: Verification gates
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Focused patch tests** (cheap-first):
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py`
2. **Full 8-file regression suite**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py tests/test_travel_guidance_ic_lora_override.py tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py`
   - Fall back to `.venv/bin/python -m pytest …` on darwin-arm64 if `uv run` hits the decord wheel gap.
3. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (executor / stub bootstrap)**: `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py::test_apply_all_wgp_patches_records_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers`. Proves the concrete runtime-model patch is reached via `apply_all_wgp_patches` and registered alongside module-level patches in the single registry — the executor-side proof for the authoritative gate.
4. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (orchestrator / full bootstrap)**: ship `scripts/sprint4/patch_lifecycle_smoke.py` that:
   - Instantiates `WanOrchestrator(wan_root=..., main_output_dir=...)` (non-smoke mode, linux).
   - After init returns, imports `get_wgp_patch_state` and prints a JSON dump.
   - Asserts `"ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"` appears in the dump with at least one `model_def:ltx2*` entry.
   - Documented in the runbook as orchestrator/linux-only (decord blocks darwin-arm64).
5. **Drift CI once-through**: handoff note to orchestrator (step 5.4); executor cannot trigger.
6. **Strict path-contract smoke**: re-run the Sprint 2 one-liner from Section 4 of the migration plan.
7. **Handoff note**: write `scripts/sprint4/_handoff/orchestrator_handoff_note.txt` that lists: staged file paths, gate summary (which gates executor ran green, which are orchestrator-owned — specifically "Drift CI runs once successfully" and any git tag/commit/push), and the two smokes (stub form passed, full form linux-only).
8. **Leave staged**: `git add` the new/changed files via Write-created paths; do NOT commit.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (audit) → Step 2 (wrapper + concrete marker patch wired into `apply_all_wgp_patches`) → Step 3 (four tests: single-registry, idempotency, context_id, bootstrap-records).
2. Steps 4 (PR drafts, 14 themes), 5 (drift CI yaml + handoff note for once-through), 6 (runbook) in parallel after Step 3.
3. Step 7 verification last; finish with handoff note.

## Validation Order
1. Focused patch tests (new wrapper + idempotency + context_id + bootstrap-records).
2. Full 8-file regression suite.
3. Strict path-contract smoke.
4. Yaml-load check for the drift workflow; dry-run the drift diff logic locally.
5. Visual review of the four drafted deliverables + handoff note + staged diff hand-off.

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Wan2GP Fork Migration — Sprint 4 (Patch Consolidation, Upstream PRs, Sustainability)

## Overview
Sprint 4 closes the migration by (a) consolidating runtime fork behavior into a single `wgp_patches.py` registry AND wiring a concrete runtime-model-definition patch into the bootstrap so `get_wgp_patch_state()` after `apply_all_wgp_patches()` proves the single-registry invariant through the real production path, (b) drafting upstream PR bodies for the 118 bucket-(b) rows grouped into the 14 literal hunk themes already present in `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv`, (c) landing a weekly drift-detection workflow per SD-008, and (d) publishing the rebase runbook per SD-007.

Key repo shape:
- `source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py` has `_register_patch_application`, `get_wgp_patch_state`, `rollback_wgp_patches`, `clear_wgp_patch_context`, and the context-scoped registry. `apply_all_wgp_patches` (`wgp_patches.py:536-580`) records seven module-level patches today.
- The authoritative idempotency precedent is `apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` (`wgp_patches.py:197-225`) which checks both registry state AND that the live patched target still carries the expected `_headless_patch_target_key` marker.
- `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py:184-222` defines `_RuntimePatchTransaction`, `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch`. No production caller today.
- Bootstrap seam: `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:265-266` (`apply_all_wgp_patches(wgp, self.wan_root)`); `wgp.models_def` is populated before this line.
- `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv`: 118 upstreamable rows, **14 distinct hunk themes** (`grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | cut -d, -f2 | sort -u | wc -l` = 14).

Environment constraints:
- Workspace default `python` is 3.8.10 and cannot import 3.10-syntax source files. **All plan-side invocations pin `.venv/bin/python` (3.10) or `uv run --python 3.10`**; no bare `python -c` is used.
- `.venv` is a minimal Python 3.10 smoke venv without pyyaml; plan adds a one-line `.venv/bin/pip install --quiet pyyaml` prep step before the yaml check (actionlint is not on PATH and no local docker image exists).
- `uv sync` fails on darwin-arm64 (decord==0.6.0 wheel gap); `source.media.structure.*` imports decord lazily, so the 8-file regression suite runs on `.venv`.
- Executor cannot write `.git/` or push remotes; orchestrator opens PRs, tags baselines, triggers `workflow_dispatch`. Real `WanOrchestrator` bootstrap imports `Wan2GP/wgp.py` which eagerly imports decord — full bootstrap smoke is orchestrator/linux-only.
- Do not commit. Stage for user review.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the patch registry, runtime primitives, bootstrap seam, and idempotency precedent (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`, `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py`, `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `wgp_patches.py:27-114` (registry + rollback primitives), `wgp_patches.py:197-225` (`apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` — authoritative **live-state + registry** idempotency guard), `wgp_patches.py:536-580` (`apply_all_wgp_patches`).
2. **Read** `runtime_registry.py:184-222` + `wgp_bridge.py:19-21,94-126` to confirm the three primitives are re-exported.
3. **Read** `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:245-267` to confirm the bootstrap seam and that `wgp.models_def` is populated before `apply_all_wgp_patches(wgp, self.wan_root)`.

### Step 2: Extend `wgp_patches.py` with a live-state-guarded runtime-model-def wrapper and a concrete bootstrap-wired marker patch (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Import** `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch` from `source.runtime.wgp_bridge`; reuse the existing `_get_runtime_for_mutation`-equivalent access via the bridge (read `runtime.models_def` through the same path used by the transaction).
2. **Implement** `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch(model_name, patch_values, *, patch_name, context_id=None)` with a guard modeled on `apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` (`wgp_patches.py:197-200`):
   - `target_key = f"model_def:{model_name}"`.
   - Snapshot live state: `runtime = _resolve_runtime(); live = dict(runtime.models_def.get(model_name, {}))`.
   - Probe registry: `registry = get_wgp_patch_state(context_id=context_id).get(patch_name, {})`.
   - **Guard A — registry + live match:** if `target_key in registry` AND `registry[target_key].get("applied")` AND `all(live.get(k) == v for k, v in patch_values.items())` → return `True` (short-circuit; existing rollback closure preserved). This answers FLAG-004 / correctness / callers.
   - **Guard B — registry stale, live drifted:** if `target_key in registry` but live values don't match, call `rollback_wgp_patches(patch_name=patch_name, target_key=target_key, context_id=context_id)` to discard the stale closure AND restore original pre-patch state. Then fall through to fresh apply.
   - **Fresh apply:** `transaction = begin_runtime_model_patch(model_name, keys=tuple(patch_values.keys())); apply_runtime_model_patch(transaction, patch_values); _register_patch_application(patch_name, target_key, applied=True, context_id=context_id, rollback=_make_runtime_model_def_rollback(transaction, len(patch_values)))`; the inner helper closes over the transaction and returns `rollback_runtime_model_patch(transaction)` then the int count.
   - Return `True`.
3. **Implement** `apply_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers_patch(wgp_module, *, context_id=None)`:
   - Iterate `[k for k in wgp_module.models_def.keys() if isinstance(k, str) and k.startswith("ltx2")]`.
   - For each, call `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch(k, {"_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch": True}, patch_name="ltx2_runtime_fork_markers", context_id=context_id)`.
   - Wrap in `try/except (RuntimeError, AttributeError, ImportError, TypeError)` → return `False` on error; return `True` on success (including the empty-ltx2-keys case, which is soft-OK for smoke mode since the runtime has no ltx2 defs to patch).
4. **Wire** into `apply_all_wgp_patches` (between existing entries and the final summary logging):
   ```python
   results["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"] = apply_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers_patch(wgp_module, context_id=context_id)
   ```
   Per-model entries the wrapper registers under `_PATCH_CONTEXT_STATE[context_id]["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"]["model_def:<key>"]` share the single registry with module-level patches; the trailing `_register_patch_application` loop also records the aggregate under the `wgp_module:<id>` target key. This answers FLAG-001 / issue_hints-1 / scope-1/2 / callers-1.
5. **Preserve** `apply_all_wgp_patches` return-dict shape and `successful`/`failed` summary logging.

### Step 3: Prove the single-registry invariant, live-state idempotency, drift-reapply, context_id scoping, and bootstrap registration (`tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_registers_in_single_registry` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`:
   - `_FakeRuntime` stub per `tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py:10-21`, monkeypatch `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module`.
   - Apply wrapper → assert live + registry state.
   - `rollback_wgp_patches(patch_name=…)` → assert key gone from both.
2. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_is_live_guard_idempotent`:
   - First apply.
   - Call wrapper again with identical args → assert guard short-circuited (no new transaction, live state unchanged, registry still has the original entry).
   - Rollback → assert restored to pre-patch (absent).
3. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_reapplies_when_live_drifted`:
   - First apply.
   - Mutate `runtime.models_def["ltx2_22B"]["_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch"] = "drifted"` (simulate external change).
   - Call wrapper again → assert the value is restored to the expected `True` (drift-reapply path ran a fresh transaction).
   - Rollback → assert the key is absent (original pre-patch state, not `"drifted"`). This pins FLAG-004.
4. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_respects_context_id` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py`:
   - Apply wrapper under `"ctx.a"` and `"ctx.b"`, assert both contexts report the entry, `clear_wgp_patch_context("ctx.a")` isolates, `try/finally` tears down both.
5. **Add** `test_apply_all_wgp_patches_records_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py`:
   - `_make_dummy_wgp_module` with `models_def={"ltx2_22B": {}, "ltx2_22B_distilled": {}, "t2v": {}}`.
   - Monkeypatch module-level patch helpers → `True`, and `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module` → stub runtime sharing the same `models_def`.
   - Call `apply_all_wgp_patches(dummy, ".", context_id="test.bootstrap")`.
   - Assert `get_wgp_patch_state(context_id="test.bootstrap")["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"]` has `model_def:ltx2_22B` + `model_def:ltx2_22B_distilled` and NOT `model_def:t2v`.

### Step 4: Draft bucket-(b) upstream PR bodies, one per literal CSV hunk theme (`scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Derive themes** from CSV: `grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | cut -d, -f2 | sort -u` → 14 literal `hunk` strings. Counts via `cut -d, -f2 docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | sort | uniq -c`.
2. **Create** `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/README.md`: drafts-only, `<NN>-<slug>.md` → exact CSV hunk string map, CSV cited as source of truth.
3. **Write 14 markdown files** (`01-ltx2-hotzone-drift.md` … `14-content-drift.md`) with Title / Summary / Affected files (from `grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | grep "<hunk exact>" | cut -d, -f1`) / Verification (`uv run --python 3.10 pytest` + maintainer-directed upstream smoke) / Review pointer (CSV's `manual diff review before resync`).
4. **Do not** run `gh pr create`.

### Step 5: Land drift CI per SD-008 (`.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml`:
   - `on.schedule: cron: '0 12 * * 1'` + `on.workflow_dispatch`.
   - `jobs.drift-check` on `ubuntu-latest`: checkout with submodules, compute pinned SHA (`git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD`), fetch upstream HEAD (`git ls-remote https://github.com/deepbeepmeep/Wan2GP.git HEAD | awk '{print $1}'`), add upstream remote inside submodule, fetch, compute `git -C Wan2GP rev-list --count $PINNED..$UPSTREAM`.
   - Append drift summary to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`. **Report-only**: do NOT fail on non-zero drift; fail only on git command error.
   - `permissions: { contents: read, pull-requests: read }`.
2. **Dry-run locally**: confirm `git -C Wan2GP rev-parse HEAD` matches `LOCAL_SHA=181bb71a21008032e4771e11663f33e4489c4512` from `scripts/sprint3/_handoff/orchestrator_handoff_note_2.txt`.
3. **Install pyyaml into `.venv` and validate yaml syntax**:
   ```
   .venv/bin/pip install --quiet pyyaml
   .venv/bin/python -c "import yaml; yaml.safe_load(open('.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml'))"
   ```
   This removes the tooling-availability uncertainty called out by all_locations-v2; if `.venv/bin/pip` cannot install (offline sandbox), fall back to a minimal tokenizer check:
   ```
   .venv/bin/python -c "from pathlib import Path; import re; txt=Path('.github/workflows/wan2gp-drift.yml').read_text(); assert re.search(r'(?m)^on:\s*$', txt) and 'workflow_dispatch' in txt and 'schedule' in txt and 'cron' in txt"
   ```
   Document the tokenizer-check fallback in the handoff note.
4. **Record** in the Sprint 4 handoff note (Step 7.7) that the "Drift CI runs once successfully" authoritative gate is orchestrator-owned (`workflow_dispatch` push). Closes issue_hints-2 documentation loop.

### Step 6: Publish the rebase runbook per SD-007 (`docs/wan2gp-rebase-runbook.md`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `docs/wan2gp-rebase-runbook.md` sections: Cadence (monthly + event-triggered), Pre-flight (tag + green 8-file suite), Fork rebase procedure in `banodoco/Wan2GP`, Submodule bump in `reigh-worker`, Post-bump verification (8-file suite + `.venv/bin/python`-pinned path smoke + both patch-lifecycle smoke forms), Rollback (orchestrator-only, SD-exec-no-git reference), Drift CI interpretation (including orchestrator-owned `workflow_dispatch` once-through).
2. **Cross-link** to `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` and `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`.

### Step 7: Verification gates
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Focused patch tests** (cheap-first):
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py`
2. **Full 8-file regression suite**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py tests/test_travel_guidance_ic_lora_override.py tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py`
   - Fall back to `.venv/bin/python -m pytest …` on darwin-arm64 if `uv run` hits the decord wheel gap.
3. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (executor / stub bootstrap)**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py::test_apply_all_wgp_patches_records_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers`
4. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (orchestrator / full bootstrap)**: ship `scripts/sprint4/patch_lifecycle_smoke.py` that instantiates `WanOrchestrator(…)`, then asserts `"ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"` appears in `get_wgp_patch_state()` with ≥1 `model_def:ltx2*` entry. Documented as orchestrator/linux-only.
5. **Drift CI once-through**: orchestrator-owned (Step 5.4 handoff note).
6. **Strict path-contract smoke — pinned 3.10 interpreter** (fixes FLAG-005 / issue_hints-v2):
   ```
   .venv/bin/python -c "import os, sys; from source.core.runtime_paths import ensure_wan2gp_on_path, get_wan2gp_path; ensure_wan2gp_on_path(); assert os.path.abspath(str(get_wan2gp_path())) in [os.path.abspath(p) for p in sys.path]; assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_handler.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_main.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py'); print('path-contract smoke OK')"
   ```
   (Bare `python` is 3.8.10 in this workspace and would fail on 3.10-only annotations in `source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py` — `.venv/bin/python` is 3.10.)
7. **Handoff note** `scripts/sprint4/_handoff/orchestrator_handoff_note.txt`:
   - Staged file paths.
   - Gate summary (executor-green vs orchestrator-owned).
   - Explicit orchestrator-owned list: "Drift CI runs once successfully via `workflow_dispatch`", baseline-tag / commit / push, PR creation against `deepbeepmeep/Wan2GP`.
   - Both smokes status (stub form executed; full form linux-only).
   - If pyyaml install was blocked, note the tokenizer fallback was used for yaml check.
8. **Leave staged**: `git add` non-`.git/` Write-created paths; do NOT commit.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (audit) → Step 2 (wrapper with live-state guard + concrete marker patch wired into `apply_all_wgp_patches`) → Step 3 (five tests: single-registry, live-guard idempotency, drift-reapply, context_id, bootstrap-records).
2. Steps 4 (14 PR drafts), 5 (drift CI yaml + pyyaml prep + handoff), 6 (runbook) in parallel after Step 3.
3. Step 7 verification last; finish with handoff note.

## Validation Order
1. Focused patch tests (5 new tests incl. live-guard + drift-reapply + context_id + bootstrap).
2. Full 8-file regression suite.
3. Strict path-contract smoke via pinned `.venv/bin/python`.
4. Yaml-load check for drift workflow (`pyyaml` install or tokenizer fallback); dry-run drift diff locally.
5. Visual review of four drafted deliverables + handoff note + staged diff hand-off.

        Robustness level:
        standard

        Requirements:
        - Decide exactly one of: PROCEED, ITERATE, ESCALATE, TIEBREAKER.
        - Use the weighted score, flag details (including `evidence`), plan delta, recurring critiques, and preflight results as judgment context.
        - PROCEED when execution should move forward now.
        - ITERATE when revising the plan is the best next move.
        - ESCALATE when the loop is stuck, churn is recurring, or user intervention is needed.
        - TIEBREAKER when a flag group reflects an *unresolvable constraint tension* (architectural or philosophical — requires a human call) rather than a plan-quality issue. Use TIEBREAKER only when the Iteration Pressure Analysis shows `addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2` for a fuzzy group OR the group has >=2 member flags across >=2 iterations. If the concern is simply that the plan writer hasn't tried hard enough, use ITERATE instead. When recommending TIEBREAKER you MUST provide `tiebreaker_question` (the decision question for human resolution), `tiebreaker_flag_ids` (which flags this resolves), and `tiebreaker_fuzzy_group_id` (which group this resolves). Cite specific flag IDs and iterations in your rationale.
        - `signals_assessment`: one paragraph summarizing score trajectory, flag status, and preflight posture.

        Flags come in two tiers:
        - **Blocking** (severity = significant/likely-significant): These are serious concerns. If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a `flag_resolutions` entry for every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance.
        - **Noted** (everything else): Acknowledge in your rationale but they don't block PROCEED.

        If there are blocking flags and you want to PROCEED, provide `flag_resolutions` with one entry per blocking flag. If you cannot resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision) or ESCALATE (human intervention needed).
        Structurally unresolvable flags (for example, infrastructure outside the repo or product decisions that require a human) are ESCALATE, not PROCEED with a non-answer.

        For each blocking flag:
        - **dispute**: The critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite something specific (file path, line, API doc, etc.). Generic statements like "handled correctly" are invalid.
        - **accept_tradeoff**: The concern is real but intentionally accepted as a known limitation. Rationale must be specific to this flag. Boilerplate like "acceptable within scope" is invalid.
        - Schema requirement: every `flag_resolutions` entry must include both `evidence` and `rationale`. Use `""` for the field that does not apply to that action.

        If there are no blocking flags, return `flag_resolutions: []`.
        Always return `accepted_tradeoffs`; use `[]` when none apply.

        Populate `settled_decisions` with design choices that should carry into review without re-litigation. Return `[]` when there are none.

        Example:
        ```json
        {
          "recommendation": "PROCEED",
          "rationale": "Core fix is correct. Convention concern accepted.",
          "signals_assessment": "Score stable at 2.5, preflight passed, no recurring critiques.",
          "warnings": ["Verify edge case with composite moduli during execution."],
          "flag_resolutions": [
            {"flag_id": "correctness-1", "action": "dispute", "evidence": "allow_migrate and allow_migrate_model produce identical behavior for this use case (verified at django/db/utils.py:286).", "rationale": ""},
            {"flag_id": "performance-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Cold-start latency remains 40ms above target because the cache warmup job is owned by platform and outside this repo; rollout is still approved for the limited internal beta."},
            {"flag_id": "conventions-1", "action": "accept_tradeoff", "evidence": "", "rationale": "Minor naming inconsistency is confined to this helper and would create churn across generated fixtures; track it as follow-up cleanup instead of blocking this fix."}
          ],
          "accepted_tradeoffs": [],
          "settled_decisions": []
        }
        ```

> AGENT

Analyzing iteration 3:

- Score trajectory: 17.5 → 22.5 → **24.5** (worsening across three iterations).
- 5 flags resolved across iterations, but each round uncovers *deeper* concerns (not reopens — reopened_count = 0 everywhere).
- System itself flagged "Scope creep detected: the plan appears to be expanding beyond the original idea."

The new v3 open flags split into two distinct threads:

**Thread A — tractable plan-quality gap (FG-002, FG-004, correctness-v3, FLAG-006, callers-1-v3, all_locations-v3)**: the live-state guard and tests cover only `runtime.models_def`, but the underlying `_RuntimePatchTransaction` (runtime_registry.py:184-222) also patches `runtime.wan_model.model_def` when `transformer_type == model_name`. This is a real gap but the planner can fix it by extending the guard + tests to probe both targets — no human input needed.

**Thread B — architectural tension (FG-003, FG-005, callers-2-v3, FLAG-007, scope-v3)**: `_PATCH_CONTEXT_STATE` / `_PATCH_CONTEXT_ROLLBACKS` are keyed by context, but the underlying WGP runtime is a single globally-shared object. Applying the same runtime patch under `ctx.a` then `ctx.b` gives `ctx.b` a rollback closure whose snapshot already contains the patched value — rolling back `ctx.b` preserves the marker instead of restoring the original. This is not solvable by "trying harder"; it requires a design decision on whether `context_id` provides true isolation for runtime patches, is bookkeeping-only, or whether cross-context runtime patches are explicitly unsupported in Sprint 4 scope.

**Iteration Pressure Analysis** shows **FG-005 has 2 member flags across 4 iterations** — which satisfies the TIEBREAKER threshold (">=2 member flags across >=2 iterations"). The planner has addressed adjacent concerns each round, but the core context-semantics-vs-global-runtime tension keeps generating new flags because no one has decided what the semantic contract actually is.

This is the textbook TIEBREAKER scenario: an architectural ambiguity that will keep generating correctness flags regardless of how hard the planner tries, because the answer isn't discoverable from code alone.

> AGENT

Recommendation: **TIEBREAKER** on FG-005 (context_id semantics for runtime-model patches).

**Why tiebreaker, not iterate**: Score monotonically worsening across three iterations (17.5 → 22.5 → 24.5), scope-creep warning emitted by the system, and FG-005 has **2 flags across 4 iterations** — satisfying the TIEBREAKER threshold. The underlying issue is architectural: `_PATCH_CONTEXT_STATE` and `_PATCH_CONTEXT_ROLLBACKS` are keyed per context_id (scoped bookkeeping), but `_RuntimePatchTransaction` mutates a single globally-shared WGP runtime. Applying the same runtime patch under two contexts means the second rollback closure snapshots an already-patched value and cannot restore the original. The planner has been treating `context_id` as a first-class isolation feature for runtime patches and keeps generating correctness concerns; the question of whether that's the right semantic requires a product/architecture decision, not more planning effort.

**The tiebreaker question** offers three concrete options:
- (A) Bookkeeping-only — raise on cross-context reuse, single-context tests only
- (B) True isolation — extend `_RuntimePatchTransaction` to track pre-patch state centrally (widens Sprint 4 scope)
- (C) Defer to follow-up sprint — single-context guarantees only, remove the cross-context test, document multi-context as unsupported

Sprint 4's only production caller is `apply_all_wgp_patches` with a single default context, so (C) is the cheapest answer and most aligned with the sprint's stated deliverables.

**Orthogonal plan-quality gap still needs one more revision after the human decides**: the live-state guard (Step 2) and its tests must also cover `runtime.wan_model.model_def` (FG-002/FG-004/FLAG-006) to match `_RuntimePatchTransaction`'s dual-target contract. That gap is tractable but should be closed in the same revision that implements the tiebreaker answer.

        # Implementation Plan: Wan2GP Fork Migration — Sprint 4 (Patch Consolidation, Upstream PRs, Sustainability)

## Overview
Sprint 4 closes the migration by (a) consolidating runtime fork behavior into a single `wgp_patches.py` registry AND wiring a concrete runtime-model-definition patch into the bootstrap so `get_wgp_patch_state()` after `apply_all_wgp_patches()` proves the single-registry invariant through the real production path, (b) drafting upstream PR bodies for the 118 bucket-(b) rows grouped into the 14 literal hunk themes already present in `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv`, (c) landing a weekly drift-detection workflow per SD-008, and (d) publishing the rebase runbook per SD-007.

**Explicit scope decision (resolves iter-3 architectural tension):** Sprint 4 uses a single default `context_id` for the production `apply_all_wgp_patches` call. Multi-context concurrent runtime-patch isolation (where the same model is patched under two different context_ids against a shared WGP runtime) is **out of scope** and deferred to a follow-up sprint. The rationale: `context_id` scopes only the registry bookkeeping; the underlying `runtime.models_def` / `runtime.wan_model.model_def` live targets are globally shared. Fixing that is a semantic redesign (runtime isolation or copy-on-write per context) that is not a Sprint 4 deliverable per `docs/WAN2GP_FORK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md:163-187`. The plan therefore (i) does not ship a test that exercises same-model multi-context apply, (ii) the bootstrap-records test exercises the non-default context_id registry path without triggering the shared-runtime pitfall, and (iii) the runbook flags the deferred work for follow-up.

Key repo shape:
- `source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py` has `_register_patch_application`, `get_wgp_patch_state`, `rollback_wgp_patches`, `clear_wgp_patch_context`, and the context-scoped registry. `apply_all_wgp_patches` (`wgp_patches.py:536-580`) records seven module-level patches today.
- Authoritative idempotency precedent: `apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` (`wgp_patches.py:197-225`) checks registry state AND the live patched target still carries the expected marker.
- `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py:184-222` — `_RuntimePatchTransaction.__init__` (lines 189-192) writes to **two** live targets: `runtime.models_def.setdefault(model_name, {})` (always) AND `runtime.wan_model.model_def` (when `runtime.transformer_type == model_name and runtime.wan_model is not None`). The wrapper guard and tests must cover BOTH targets to match this contract.
- Bootstrap seam: `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:265-266`; `wgp.models_def` is populated before this line.
- `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv`: 118 upstreamable rows, 14 distinct hunk themes.

Environment constraints:
- Workspace default `python` is 3.8.10. **All plan-side invocations pin `.venv/bin/python` (3.10) or `uv run --python 3.10`.**
- `.venv` has no pyyaml; Step 5.3 installs pyyaml with a regex-tokenizer fallback.
- `uv sync` fails on darwin-arm64 (decord wheel gap); the 8-file regression suite runs on `.venv`.
- Executor cannot write `.git/` or push remotes. Real `WanOrchestrator` bootstrap is orchestrator/linux-only.
- Do not commit. Stage for user review.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the patch registry, runtime primitives, bootstrap seam, and dual-target transaction contract (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`, `source/runtime/wgp_ports/runtime_registry.py`, `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Read** `wgp_patches.py:27-114` (registry + rollback primitives), `wgp_patches.py:197-225` (`apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` — authoritative live-state + registry idempotency guard), `wgp_patches.py:536-580` (`apply_all_wgp_patches`).
2. **Read** `runtime_registry.py:184-222` — confirm `_RuntimePatchTransaction` targets BOTH `runtime.models_def[model_name]` AND `runtime.wan_model.model_def` (when `transformer_type == model_name`). Confirm `wgp_bridge.py:19-21,94-126` re-exports the three primitives.
3. **Read** `source/models/wgp/orchestrator.py:245-267` — confirm the bootstrap seam and that `wgp.models_def` is populated before `apply_all_wgp_patches(wgp, self.wan_root)`; confirm only one production caller exists, with a default `context_id`.

### Step 2: Extend `wgp_patches.py` with a dual-target-aware runtime-model-def wrapper and a concrete bootstrap-wired marker patch (`source/models/wgp/wgp_patches.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Import** `begin_runtime_model_patch`, `apply_runtime_model_patch`, `rollback_runtime_model_patch` from `source.runtime.wgp_bridge`.
2. **Implement** `apply_runtime_model_definition_patch(model_name, patch_values, *, patch_name, context_id=None)` with a **dual-target** live-state guard modeled on `apply_qwen_lora_directory_patch` (`wgp_patches.py:197-200`) AND `_RuntimePatchTransaction.__init__` (`runtime_registry.py:189-192`):
   - `target_key = f"model_def:{model_name}"`.
   - Resolve the live runtime via the bridge. Snapshot BOTH targets the transaction would touch:
     - `live_models_def = runtime.models_def.get(model_name, {})`.
     - `is_active = getattr(runtime, "transformer_type", None) == model_name and getattr(runtime, "wan_model", None) is not None`.
     - If `is_active`: `live_loaded = runtime.wan_model.model_def`; else `live_loaded = None`.
   - Probe registry: `registry = get_wgp_patch_state(context_id=context_id).get(patch_name, {})`.
   - **Guard A — registry + BOTH live targets match:** if `target_key in registry` AND `registry[target_key].get("applied")` AND `all(live_models_def.get(k) == v for k, v in patch_values.items())` AND (if `is_active`: `all(live_loaded.get(k) == v for k, v in patch_values.items())`) → return `True` (short-circuit). This addresses FLAG-006 / correctness-v3 / callers-1-v3 / all_locations-v3.
   - **Guard B — registry says applied but any live target drifted:** call `rollback_wgp_patches(patch_name=patch_name, target_key=target_key, context_id=context_id)` to discard the stale closure AND restore the original pre-patch state the existing transaction captured, then fall through to fresh apply.
   - **Fresh apply:** `transaction = begin_runtime_model_patch(model_name, keys=tuple(patch_values.keys()))` — this snapshot now captures BOTH targets per lines 189-198 of runtime_registry. `apply_runtime_model_patch(transaction, patch_values)` writes BOTH targets per line 210. Register via `_register_patch_application(patch_name, target_key, applied=True, context_id=context_id, rollback=_make_runtime_model_def_rollback(transaction, ...))`; inner helper calls `rollback_runtime_model_patch(transaction)` — which restores BOTH targets per lines 213-222 — and returns an int count.
   - Return `True`.
   - **Docstring note:** "Sprint 4 production usage is a single default `context_id`. Multi-context concurrent application against a shared WGP runtime is NOT safe with current semantics and is deferred to a follow-up sprint."
3. **Implement** `apply_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers_patch(wgp_module, *, context_id=None)`: iterate `[k for k in wgp_module.models_def.keys() if isinstance(k, str) and k.startswith("ltx2")]`, call the wrapper for each with `{"_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch": True}` and `patch_name="ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"`. `try/except (RuntimeError, AttributeError, ImportError, TypeError)` → `False`; else `True` (empty-ltx2 case soft-OK).
4. **Wire** into `apply_all_wgp_patches` (between existing entries and the trailing summary-registration loop):
   ```python
   results["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"] = apply_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers_patch(wgp_module, context_id=context_id)
   ```
5. **Preserve** `apply_all_wgp_patches` return-dict shape and `successful`/`failed` summary logging.

### Step 3: Prove single-registry, dual-target live-guard, drift-reapply, active-model coverage, and bootstrap registration (`tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`, `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_registers_in_single_registry` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py`:
   - `_FakeRuntime` stub per `tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py:10-21` with `transformer_type=None` (inactive model); monkeypatch `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module`.
   - Apply wrapper → assert `runtime.models_def[model_name][key] == expected` AND registry state.
   - `rollback_wgp_patches(patch_name=…)` → assert key gone from `models_def` AND registry.
2. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_is_dual_target_live_guard_idempotent`:
   - `_FakeRuntime` with `transformer_type=model_name` AND populated `wan_model.model_def` (active-model case).
   - First apply → assert BOTH `runtime.models_def[...]` AND `runtime.wan_model.model_def[...]` hold the expected value.
   - Second call with identical args → assert guard short-circuited: live state unchanged on BOTH targets, registry still has the original entry.
   - Rollback → assert BOTH targets restored to pre-patch (key absent or original value).
3. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_reapplies_when_models_def_drifts`:
   - Active-model setup; first apply.
   - Mutate `runtime.models_def[...]["_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch"] = "drifted_mdef"` → call wrapper again → assert models_def value restored to `True` (drift-reapply ran).
   - Rollback → assert pre-patch state restored on BOTH targets (not the drifted value).
4. **Add** `test_runtime_model_definition_patch_reapplies_when_wan_model_drifts`:
   - Active-model setup; first apply.
   - Mutate `runtime.wan_model.model_def["_banodoco_fork_runtime_patch"] = "drifted_loaded"` (leaving `models_def` untouched) → call wrapper again → assert `wan_model.model_def` value restored to `True` AND `models_def` is also still `True` (dual-target guard re-applies whenever EITHER target drifts). This directly pins FLAG-006.
   - Rollback → assert BOTH targets back to pre-patch.
5. **Add** `test_apply_all_wgp_patches_records_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers` in `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py` (sits next to existing `test_patch_state_is_context_scoped`):
   - `_make_dummy_wgp_module` with `models_def={"ltx2_22B": {}, "ltx2_22B_distilled": {}, "t2v": {}}`.
   - Monkeypatch module-level patch helpers → `True`, and `runtime_registry.get_wgp_runtime_module` → stub runtime sharing that `models_def` with `transformer_type=None` (so the bootstrap exercise stays on the inactive-model branch — the active-model branch is covered by tests 2-4).
   - Call `apply_all_wgp_patches(dummy, ".", context_id="test.bootstrap")` — exercises the non-default `context_id` registry path.
   - Assert `get_wgp_patch_state(context_id="test.bootstrap")["ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"]` contains `model_def:ltx2_22B` + `model_def:ltx2_22B_distilled` and NOT `model_def:t2v`.
   - Tear down `clear_wgp_patch_context("test.bootstrap")` in a `try/finally`.
6. **Do NOT** add a same-model cross-context test. The shared-runtime multi-context tension (FLAG-007 / callers-2-v3 / scope-v3) is explicitly out of Sprint 4 scope per the Overview's scope decision.

If multi-context production runtime patches become needed, follow-up work must either make `context_id` drive a per-context runtime view (copy-on-write against `runtime.models_def`) or forbid same-model runtime patches under differing contexts." Cross-reference the iter-3 gate's FLAG-007 / scope-v3.
3. **Cross-link** to `docs/wan2gp-triage.csv` and `scripts/sprint4/upstream_prs/`.

### Step 7: Verification gates
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Focused patch tests** (cheap-first):
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_lifecycle.py tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py tests/test_runtime_model_patch_contracts.py`
2. **Full 8-file regression suite**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_travel_guidance_config.py tests/test_travel_ltx_vpt.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_travel.py tests/test_ltx_hybrid_vgkfi.py tests/test_travel_payload_contracts.py tests/test_travel_orchestrator_terminal_gating.py tests/test_travel_guidance_ic_lora_override.py tests/test_task_registry_ic_lora_dedup.py`
   - Fall back to `.venv/bin/python -m pytest …` on darwin-arm64 if `uv run` hits the decord wheel gap.
3. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (executor / stub bootstrap)**:
   `uv run --python 3.10 pytest tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py::test_apply_all_wgp_patches_records_ltx2_runtime_fork_markers`
4. **Patch-lifecycle smoke (orchestrator / full bootstrap)**: ship `scripts/sprint4/patch_lifecycle_smoke.py` that instantiates `WanOrchestrator(…)` and asserts `"ltx2_runtime_fork_markers"` appears in `get_wgp_patch_state()` with ≥1 `model_def:ltx2*` entry. Orchestrator/linux-only.
5. **Drift CI once-through**: orchestrator-owned.
6. **Strict path-contract smoke — pinned 3.10 interpreter**:
   ```
   .venv/bin/python -c "import os, sys; from source.core.runtime_paths import ensure_wan2gp_on_path, get_wan2gp_path; ensure_wan2gp_on_path(); assert os.path.abspath(str(get_wan2gp_path())) in [os.path.abspath(p) for p in sys.path]; assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_handler.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/qwen/qwen_main.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/loras_mutipliers.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/shared/utils/self_refiner.py'); assert os.path.isfile('Wan2GP/models/ltx2/ltx_core/tools.py'); print('path-contract smoke OK')"
   ```
7. **Handoff note** `scripts/sprint4/_handoff/orchestrator_handoff_note.txt`:
   - Staged file paths.
   - Executor-green vs orchestrator-owned gate summary.
   - Orchestrator-owned list: "Drift CI runs once successfully via `workflow_dispatch`", baseline-tag / commit / push, PR creation.
   - Both smokes status.
   - Which yaml-check path was used (pyyaml install or tokenizer fallback).
   - **Explicit Sprint 5 backlog item**: multi-context runtime-patch isolation is deferred (deliberate Sprint 4 scope decision; see runbook Sprint-5 followup section).
8. **Leave staged**: `git add` non-`.git/` Write-created paths; do NOT commit.

## Execution Order
1. Step 1 (audit) → Step 2 (wrapper with dual-target live-state guard + concrete marker patch wired into `apply_all_wgp_patches`) → Step 3 (five tests: single-registry, dual-target idempotency, models_def-drift-reapply, wan_model-drift-reapply, bootstrap-records).
2. Steps 4 (14 PR drafts), 5 (drift CI yaml + pyyaml prep + handoff), 6 (runbook with Sprint 5 followup section) in parallel after Step 3.
3. Step 7 verification last; finish with handoff note.

## Validation Order
1. Focused patch tests (5 new tests, all covering dual-target semantics where applicable).
2. Full 8-file regression suite.
3. Strict path-contract smoke via pinned `.venv/bin/python`.
4. Yaml-load check for drift workflow (`pyyaml` install or tokenizer fallback); dry-run drift diff locally.
5. Visual review of four drafted deliverables + handoff note + staged diff hand-off.

        "resolution": "Addressed every significant flag from gate iteration 1. (3) all_locations-1: Corrected the theme count from ~13 to exactly 14, derived from `grep ',upstreamable,' docs/wan2gp-triage.csv | cut -d, -f2 | sort -u`; PR drafts now use literal CSV hunk strings instead of invented groupings. (4) all_locations-2: Added a context_id-scoped test in `tests/test_wgp_patch_context_contracts.py` that applies the wrapper under two distinct contexts and verifies `clear_wgp_patch_context` isolation. Success criteria updated to name the concrete marker patch and count themes as 14."

        "resolution": "Addressed the six new open flags from iteration-2 critique (the iteration-1 flags remain addressed in plan_v2 and are not re-edited).         "concern": "Runtime patch state: the revised live-state guard checks only `runtime.models_def`, but the actual transaction also patches the active loaded model (`runtime.wan_model.model_def`), so split live state can go undetected.",
        "resolution": "Addressed the three open iter-3 correctness flags and took option (C) on the architectural tension flagged for tiebreaker. (1) FLAG-006 / correctness-v3 / callers-1-v3 / all_locations-v3 (dual-target live-state coverage): the guard now snapshots BOTH `runtime.models_def[model_name]` AND `runtime.wan_model.model_def` (when `transformer_type == model_name and wan_model is not None`), matching `_RuntimePatchTransaction.__init__` at `runtime_registry.py:189-192`. Guard short-circuits only when registry AND both live targets match; on drift of EITHER target, it runs `rollback_wgp_patches` then a fresh transaction. Tests 2-4 now exercise the active-model case (transformer_type == model_name with a populated wan_model.model_def): dual-target idempotency, drift in models_def reapplies, drift in wan_model.model_def reapplies. (2) FLAG-007 / callers-2-v3 / scope-v3 (cross-context shared-runtime isolation): explicitly scoped out of Sprint 4 in the Overview, documented as a deferred follow-up in the new runbook Sprint-5 section (Step 6.2), and called out in the handoff note (Step 7.7). The problematic same-model cross-context test is REMOVED; the bootstrap-records test already covers the non-default `context_id` registry path via `context_id=\"test.bootstrap\"`, which is all that Sprint 4's single-production-caller shape requires. Rationale: the gate rationale itself notes option (C) \"defer multi-context semantics\" is the correct call, Sprint 4 deliverables do not require cross-context runtime isolation, and shipping a test that quietly accepts broken semantics would be worse than deferring. Success criteria updated: context_id scoping criterion replaced with dual-target live-state coverage; tests criterion renumbered a-e. Plan scope tightened back to Sprint 4 deliverables per the scope-creep warning."
